```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Per-Class Performance Breakdown}\n\\label{sec:per_class}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:per_class} reports per-class precision, recall, and F1 for all experimental conditions.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-class precision, recall, and F1 for all experimental conditions (seed 42). DeepSeek VOTA achieves 100\\% fail precision but only 45.43\\% fail recall, indicating systematic bias toward predicting \\texttt{pass}. DeepSeek \\texttt{exception\\_only} seed 42 achieves 100\\% fail recall, but seed 123 drops to 70.5\\% (see Table~\\ref{tab:full_deepseek}).}\n\\label{tab:per_class}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{llccccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Acc} & \\textbf{Pass P} & \\textbf{Pass R} & \\textbf{Pass F1} & \\textbf{Fail P} & \\textbf{Fail R} & \\textbf{Fail F1} \\\\\n\\midrule\nNo Trace          & Qwen-2.5-7B & 75.13 & 87.76 & 75.79 & 81.34 & 54.73 & 73.45 & 62.72 \\\\\nFull Trace        & Qwen-2.5-7B & 81.76 & 87.74 & 86.60 & 87.17 & 67.43 & 69.62 & 68.51 \\\\\nFull Trace (LO)   & Qwen-2.5-7B & 86.22 & 88.21 & 93.18 & 90.63 & 80.07 & 68.73 & 73.97 \\\\\nVOTA              & Qwen-2.5-7B & 99.41 & 100.00 & 99.18 & 99.59 & 97.98 & 100.00 & 98.98 \\\\\nException Only    & Qwen-2.5-7B & 99.83 & 100.00 & 99.76 & 99.88 & 99.41 & 100.00 & 99.71 \\\\\nAE Only           & Qwen-2.5-7B & 99.92 & 100.00 & 99.88 & 99.94 & 99.71 & 100.00 & 99.85 \\\\\nLoop Only         & Qwen-2.5-7B & 85.63 & 92.39 & 87.07 & 89.66 & 71.65 & 82.01 & 76.48 \\\\\nVOTA$-$AE         & Qwen-2.5-7B & 99.41 & 99.88 & 99.29 & 99.59 & 98.26 & 99.71 & 98.98 \\\\\n\\midrule\nNo Trace          & DeepSeek-6.7B & 73.36 & 81.71 & 80.85 & 81.28 & 53.16 & 54.57 & 53.86 \\\\\nVOTA              & DeepSeek-6.7B & 84.45 & 82.14 & 100.00 & 90.20 & 100.00 & 45.43 & 62.47 \\\\\nException Only    & DeepSeek-6.7B & 99.33 & 100.00 & 99.06 & 99.53 & 97.69 & 100.00 & 98.83 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{sec:training_details}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:hyperparams} summarizes the training hyperparameters shared across all conditions.\nAll experiments use the HuggingFace TRL \\texttt{SFTTrainer} with LoRA \\citep{hu2022lora} applied to all linear layers.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters (shared across all conditions).}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Hyperparameter} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA rank ($r$)            & 16 \\\\\nLoRA alpha ($\\alpha$)      & 32 \\\\\nLoRA dropout               & 0.05 \\\\\nLoRA target modules        & all linear layers \\\\\n\\midrule\nEpochs                     & 3 \\\\\nPer-device batch size       & 4 \\\\\nGradient accumulation steps & 8 \\\\\nEffective batch size        & 32 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLearning rate               & $2 \\times 10^{-4}$ \\\\\nLR scheduler                & linear decay \\\\\nWarmup ratio                & 0.1 \\\\\nWeight decay                & 0.01 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMax sequence length         & 2048 tokens \\\\\nPrimary seed                & 42 \\\\\nRobustness seeds            & 123, 456 \\\\\n\\midrule\nHardware                    & 1$\\times$ RTX PRO 6000 \\\\\n                            & Blackwell (94\\,GB VRAM) \\\\\nFramework                   & HuggingFace TRL SFTTrainer \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:train_time} reports per-condition training wall-clock times.\nConditions with longer trace inputs (e.g., \\texttt{full\\_trace}, VOTA) require proportionally more training time due to increased sequence lengths.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training wall-clock time per condition on a single RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPU. Where multiple seeds were run, individual times are listed.}\n\\label{tab:train_time}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{llr}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed(s)} & \\textbf{Time (h)} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{no\\_trace}       & 42         & ${\\sim}$0.5 \\\\\n\\texttt{full\\_trace}     & 42         & ${\\sim}$2.2 \\\\\n\\texttt{label\\_only}     & 42         & ${\\sim}$2.2 \\\\\nVOTA                     & 42         & ${\\sim}$2.3 \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 42         & ${\\sim}$2.1 \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 123        & ${\\sim}$2.1 \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 456        & ${\\sim}$1.3 \\\\\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}        & 42         & ${\\sim}$1.8 \\\\\n\\texttt{loop\\_only}      & 42         & ${\\sim}$2.1 \\\\\n\\texttt{VOTA\\_no\\_ae}    & 42         & ${\\sim}$1.8 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{VOTA Trace Examples}\n\\label{sec:vota_examples}\n\nWe provide two representative VOTA trace examples illustrating the fail and pass cases.\nIn the fail case, the exception event provides an unambiguous fault signal.\nIn the pass case, the absence of an exception event combined with a matching assertion point confirms correct execution.\n\n\\paragraph{Fail example.}\nA \\texttt{ZeroDivisionError} triggers an \\texttt{[ERROR]} event, providing a direct fault signal that is sufficient for the model to predict \\texttt{fail}.\n\n\\begin{small}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n## Function\ndef divide(a, b):\n    return a / b\n\n## Test Input\ndivide(1, 0)\n\n## Expected Output\ninf\n\n## VOTA Trace\n[ERROR] ZeroDivisionError: division by zero\n\n## Verdict\nfail\n\\end{verbatim}\n\\end{small}\n\n\\paragraph{Pass example.}\nNo exception is raised, and the assertion point confirms that the actual output matches the expected value.\n\n\\begin{small}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n## Function\ndef add(a, b):\n    return a + b\n\n## Test Input\nadd(2, 3)\n\n## Expected Output\n5\n\n## VOTA Trace\n[LOOP] No loops detected.\n[ASSERT] actual=5, expected=5\n\n## Verdict\npass\n\\end{verbatim}\n\\end{small}\n\nThese examples illustrate the core asymmetry exploited by the \\texttt{exception\\_only} condition: fail cases produce an explicit \\texttt{[ERROR]} event, while pass cases are characterized by its absence.\nThis binary signal---presence vs.\\ absence of an exception---is sufficient for near-perfect verdict prediction.\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{Full Experimental Results}\n\\label{sec:full_results}\n\nTables~\\ref{tab:full_qwen} and \\ref{tab:full_deepseek} report the complete experimental results across all conditions, models, and seeds.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Full results for Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B across all conditions and seeds. Conditions with a single seed report no variance. \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieves 99.83\\% across all three seeds with zero variance.}\n\\label{tab:full_qwen}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{S42} & \\textbf{S123} & \\textbf{S456} & \\textbf{Mean $\\pm$ Std} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 99.83 & 99.83 & 99.83 & 99.83 $\\pm$ 0.00 \\\\\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}        & 99.92 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\nVOTA                     & 99.41 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\texttt{VOTA\\_no\\_ae}    & 99.41 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\texttt{label\\_only}     & 86.22 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\texttt{loop\\_only}      & 85.63 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\texttt{full\\_trace}     & 81.76 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\texttt{no\\_trace}       & 75.13 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Full results for DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B across all conditions and seeds. \\texttt{exception\\_only} shows bimodal seed behavior: seeds 42 and 456 achieve 99.33\\%, while seed 123 yields 91.09\\% (LoRA initialization outlier, fail recall = 70.5\\%).}\n\\label{tab:full_deepseek}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{Accuracy} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 42  & 99.33 & epoch 1: 99.83 \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 456 & 99.33 & CI [98.82, 99.75] \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 123 & 91.09 & fail recall = 70.5\\%; CI [89.40, 92.60] \\\\\n\\midrule\nVOTA                     & 42  & 84.45 & \\\\\n\\texttt{no\\_trace}       & 42  & 73.36 & \\\\\n\\texttt{full\\_trace}     & 42  & 79.16 & CI [76.80, 81.30] \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{Irreducible Sample Analysis}\n\\label{sec:irreducible}\n\nThe \\texttt{exception\\_only} condition with Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B misclassifies exactly 2 out of 1,190 test samples across all three seeds (42, 123, 456), with 100\\% overlap across seeds.\nBoth samples (IDs 901 and 905) share the following characteristics:\n\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\textbf{Function:} \\texttt{check\\_dict\\_case} (HumanEval/95)---a function that checks whether all dictionary keys are uniformly upper-case or lower-case strings.\n\\item \\textbf{Ground-truth label:} \\texttt{pass}.\n\\item \\textbf{Predicted label:} \\texttt{fail} (false negative).\n\\item \\textbf{Cross-seed consistency:} Both samples are misclassified identically by all three seeds, indicating a systematic rather than stochastic error.\n\\end{itemize}\n\nManual inspection reveals the root cause: \\texttt{check\\_dict\\_case} uses \\texttt{all()} over a generator expression, and Python's \\texttt{all()} short-circuits on the first \\texttt{True} value, raising \\texttt{GeneratorExit} to terminate the generator.\nBecause \\texttt{GeneratorExit} is a \\texttt{BaseException} subclass, the tracer records it as seven \\texttt{[ERROR] GeneratorExit} lines---even though the function executes correctly and the test passes.\nThe model observes these spurious error markers and reasonably but incorrectly predicts \\texttt{fail}.\nThis is not a model failure but a representation ceiling: the \\texttt{exception\\_only} format cannot distinguish genuine exceptions from benign \\texttt{BaseException} subclasses triggered by Python's iterator protocol.\n\nThese 2 irreducible errors establish a practical accuracy ceiling of $1 - 2/1190 = 99.83\\%$ for the \\texttt{exception\\_only} condition on this dataset, suggesting that further improvements would require either supplementary trace signals or modifications to the input representation.\n",
```

```text
30	\caption{Cross-model accuracy ($N{=}1{,}190$). Qwen CIs: exception\_only [99.58, 100.00], VOTA [98.82, 99.83], full\_trace [79.70, 83.90], no\_trace [72.77, 77.48]; DeepSeek CIs: full\_trace [76.80, 81.30], VOTA [82.60, 86.40], no\_trace [70.80, 75.70]. $^\dag$DeepSeek \texttt{exception\_only} 3-seed results: 99.33\% (seeds 42, 456; CI [98.82, 99.75]) and 91.09\% (seed 123; CI [89.40, 92.60]); seed 123 is a LoRA initialization outlier (fail recall = 70.5\% vs.\ 100\%). $^\S$VOTA\_no\_ae evaluated on Qwen only; CI [98.90, 99.80].}
31	\label{tab:cross_model}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	Table~\ref{tab:cross_model} reveals a striking asymmetry.
35	Under \texttt{exception\_only}, Qwen achieves 99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00 across three seeds, while DeepSeek shows a bimodal pattern: seeds 42 and 456 both achieve 99.33\% (gap = 0.50pp), while seed 123 yields 91.09\% (gap = 8.74pp), attributable to suboptimal LoRA initialization (fail recall = 70.5\% vs.\ 100\% for other seeds).
36	Even the outlier seed outperforms VOTA (84.45\%) by 6.64pp, confirming exception events as the most effective component across models.
37	Under VOTA, the gap widens to 14.96pp: DeepSeek drops to 84.45\% while Qwen remains at 99.41\%.
38	Post-hoc tokenizer analysis reveals a confound: measured on the training set using each model's tokenizer, the DeepSeek tokenizer averages 2{,}759 tokens per VOTA sample versus 1{,}908 for Qwen, causing 91.7\% of DeepSeek VOTA training samples to exceed the 2{,}048-token context window.
39	The VOTA training framework preserves the verdict label but truncates the trace content (input), so the model trains with incomplete diagnostic information for the vast majority of the dataset.
40	The 14.96pp gap thus reflects both model capability differences and training-time trace truncation; the two factors cannot be disentangled in the current design.
41	However, a post-hoc group analysis provides directional evidence: partitioning DeepSeek VOTA test samples by whether their training-set counterparts exceeded the context window, intact samples ($N{=}99$) achieve 100\% accuracy while truncated samples ($N{=}1{,}091$) achieve 83.04\% (95\% CI [80.75\%, 85.24\%]; Fisher exact $p{<}0.001$), with all 185 errors concentrated in the fail class of the truncated group (fail recall $=$ 31.23\%).
```
